# State machines in The Network: synthesis and Quint plan

Date: 2026-11-07. Branch: `claude/quint-state-machines`, base commit a0ed7bc. This file is research only. It changes no code. Sections 1, 3, 4 and 6 are hand-written. Sections 2 and 5 are generated by a script from the 25 reports and a hand-written merge table.

**Result in numbers.** 252 machines after merge (105 P0, 77 P1, 70 P2), from 227 additional ids that several reports gave to the same machines. 510 suspected defects after merge (86 P0, 207 P1, 217 P2), from 610 report entries. 16 Quint modules are planned. 50 defects have no module: they need a corpus row, a sim gate, a lint or a doc fix.

Section 5 now carries a verdict for each defect from the refutation check (wave 1): 225 confirmed, 134 disputed, 151 refuted, 0 unverified. Report evidence levels differ: some defects were reproduced by a probe, others were found by reading only.

## 1. Method and coverage

**Readers.** 25 reports in `docs/research/state-machines/`: 16 package and area reports (`blooio-notify`, `capital`, `core`, `deploy-ops`, `engine`, `implicit-sweep`, `mcp-oauth`, `network-consent-core`, `network-harness`, `network-service-inbound`, `network-service-outbound`, `observatory`, `platform-consent`, `platform-identity`, `sim`, `sql-schema`), 3 journey reports (`journey-signup`, `journey-match`, `journey-exit-safety`) and 6 gap reports (`gap-active-member-journey`, `gap-app-journeys`, `gap-bias-monitor`, `gap-connector-journey`, `gap-console-client`, `gap-status-columns`). Total 18,384 lines, 1.35 MB.

**Coverage of this synthesis. The task asked for a full read of every file. That was not done.** A read call reported that the rest of a file did not fit in the context left, and 1.35 MB is too large to hold with the output that this task needs. So the coverage is not equal:

| Coverage level | Files | What was read |
|---|---|---|
| Read line by line, in full | `implicit-sweep`, `core`, `gap-status-columns`, `network-service-inbound`, `network-service-outbound`, `network-consent-core` | All lines. |
| Parsed by script, sections viewed | The other 19 reports | A script read the whole file and extracted the machine index and every defect block (title, locations, trigger, confidence, priority). I viewed the index tables, defect titles, and the Quint plan sections or journey outlines. I did not read their machine narratives line by line (state tables, invariants, failure analysis). |

**What was not read and why.** The machine narrative sections of those 19 reports. The reason is the context limit above. Effects:

- Sections 2 and 5 are complete for ids, because a script parsed all 25 files. A coverage check compares catalog ids with every index table.
- `~n` and `n/s` state counts in section 2 come from a script, not from a read. 100 machines have `n/s`. Treat all `~n` values as estimates. Wave 2 authors must read the owner report section.
- Duplicates can remain in section 5 among reports that were not read line by line, because the merge used titles for them. Two merges that join close but not identical defects are marked by the wider title (for example the row that joins `journey-match` M8 and `journey-exit-safety` JE18). Check these before fixing.
- Trigger text for the 19 reports is a script cut of the report text. It can stop mid-sentence, and a few rows hold the first sentence of the defect, not a true trigger. Read the source defect before acting.
- Priority: the highest stated priority of merged entries. Where a report gave none, I assigned one: `implicit-sweep` by the rule it touches; `capital` P2, or P1 and P0 for the entries that touch replay, forget, or age; `sim` P1 when its "effect on proof" column says yes or partly, else P2; `gap-bias-monitor` P1 for high severity, else P2.
- Each Quint module in section 6 reflects the Quint proposal of each report, but module authors must read `doc_refs` in the [Quint module plan](state-machines/quint-plan.json) in full.

**Limits inherited from the reports.** The Eliza gateway and Eliza Cloud are upstream and not in this repository, so statements about them are unverified. The reports' scratch probes are outside the repository. I ran no test, no model and no database for this synthesis. I did not check any file:line against the code.

## 3. Cross-machine interaction map

The table lists shared state and required ordering. Each row can break even when each machine is correct alone.

| Link | Machines | Shared state or order | Failure if the link breaks | Defects |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| X1. Consent stores | `consent-ledger`, `opt-out-projection`, `gateway-consent-mirror`, `recycled-hold` | Four stores answer "may we text this person". Ledger write comes first. Projections lag. No transaction spans them. | Stores disagree after a crash or late message. | SD-001, SD-104, SD-023, SD-130 |
| X2. Turn and effects | `signed-turn`, `consent-ledger`, `store-unit-of-work`, `text-join`, `collected-reply` | The claim commits first. Each effect commits alone. Undo runs on an exception, not on a crash. | Partial effects with a refused retry. | SD-001, SD-128, SD-011 |
| X3. Belief and truth | `send-belief`, `send-pipeline-and-deferred`, `outbound-queue-row`, `message-status-mirror`, `notify-item`, `booked-attendance` | `onSent` means "written to the queue". `storeStatuses` writes `network.messages.status` only. No code in `packages/network/src` reads it. | The Network acts on sends that never happened. | SD-006, SD-030, SD-095, SD-257 |
| X4. Deferred outbox before the queue | `send-pipeline-and-deferred`, `relay-swap`, `booked-attendance` | A deferred send sits in `network_state`, before queue admission, with no hook on refusal. | One-way number, late consent, lost reveal. | SD-007, SD-146, SD-248 |
| X5. Answer routing | `routing`, `awaiting-slot`, `opp-participant-status`, `yes-hold`, `reply-reader` | Route picks the app. The one slot picks the item. The reader picks the meaning. All three decide a consent. | A reply reaches the wrong item or reads as a yes. | SD-028, SD-077, SD-043, SD-041, SD-033 |
| X6. `dropMember` hub | `trust-level`, `safety-report`, `member-age-policy`, `opt-out-projection`, `opportunity-lifecycle`, `awaiting-slot` | Every safety machine enters consent machines through `network.ts:2564`. It has one branch per stage. | Bad notices, stale slots. | SD-047, SD-048 |
| X7. Person against member id | `person-safety-carryover`, `trust-level`, `safety-case`, `erasure-saga`, `membership`, `age-floor` | Safety state lives per (app, member). Identity lives per person. A leave mints a new member id. Age alone has a person-level store. | Hold, watch and report reach do not follow the person. | SD-021, SD-022, SD-074, SD-256 |
| X8. Sagas with no retry | `ban-saga`, `erasure-saga`, `phone-identity-hold`, `oauth-forget-revoke`, `photo-object`, `clef-rating` | Each step is its own commit, in a set order. Listeners run on some paths only. | Residue, or a saga that cannot finish. | SD-005, SD-016, SD-089, SD-052 |
| X9. Membership writers | `membership`, `web-join`, `text-join`, `ban-saga`, `opt-out-projection`, `photo-consent` | `putMembership` writes the whole row. Not all writers take the join lock. | A late writer undoes leave, ban or STOP. | SD-017, SD-127 |
| X10. Locks and latency | `store-unit-of-work`, `line-drain-lease`, `sender-serialization`, `signed-turn`, `suggestion-item` | Turn awaits unit, then drain, then line lock and remote calls. One process-wide join chain. Lock order: `network_state`, people, memberships, members, inbound. | The 8 s turn budget has no protection. | SD-096, SD-112 |
| X11. Tick wiring | `backend-tick-loop`, `retention-loop`, `legacy-inbox-row`, `line-conversation`, `stop-hint-window`, `suggestion-batch` | The production tick runs network, notify and ops only. ## 5. Suspected-defect register

**Verdicts.** 225 confirmed, 134 disputed, 151 refuted, 0 unverified (of 510).

**Method.** Two independent agents tried to refute each defect against the code at this branch; two survivals = confirmed, one = disputed, none = refuted. Refuted rows stay in the register so that later waves do not chase them. The reason in each Verdict cell comes from the votes: for SD-352, SD-353 and SD-356 to SD-510 it is hand-written; for the other rows it was extracted by a script from the vote text, and a row that says "both votes" or "one vote ... other vote" with a generic phrase has a template reason. The full votes are in `defect-verdicts.json` in the session scratchpad (not in the repo).

The ids are stable from this file on. Rows were deduplicated by hand from 610 report entries to 510 rows. Priority is the highest priority of the merged entries; where a report gave none, the rule in section 1 applies. `file:line` uses the short file names of the source report. Trigger text is copied from the report, cut to 230 characters. See the [wave 1 defect checks](state-machines/defect-checks.md) for the recovered verifier votes.

## 6. Quint model plan (wave 2)

### 6.1 Rules for all modules

- Files go under `quint/` in the repository. `quint/` does not exist yet. Wave 2 creates it.
- 16 modules, ordered P0 first by the order of risk. Each module has one file, so authors can work in parallel with no shared files.
- **No shared `quint/common` module.** It is not truly needed. Each module defines its own small types, so that no author waits for another. The cost is some repeated type text. Where two modules share a fact (for example `opted_out`), each module takes it as a nondeterministic input.
- One SQL transaction is one atomic action. Each `await` between transactions is a possible crash and interleave point. Row locks are implicit.
- Regex readers, the leak guard, HMAC, Clef and matching scores are oracles. Quint checks what a misread can reach. Corpus rows test the readers. Quint will not find defects in the reading itself (SD-031, SD-032, SD-034, SD-033).
- Time is an integer tick with named thresholds. Skew is a bounded offset per side. Ids and hashes are small ids.
- The gateway is a nondeterministic retrier. Each assumption about upstream is written as an assumption, not as a fact.
- Each module has at least one reachability witness, so that a vacuous pass is visible.
- Before each invariant, state the rule it comes from (AGENTS.md item, PRD section). For a defect the model reproduces, set the Verdict in section 5. A reproduced defect needs an integration, e2e, sim gate or corpus case for its fix. The repo has no unit or smoke tests (docs/tests-policy.md).


### 6.2 `quint/turn_consent.qnt` (P0)

- **Machines (19).** `signed-turn`, `sender-serialization`, `consent-ledger`, `gateway-consent-mirror`, `opt-out-projection`, `optout-reader`, `stop-hint-window`, `agent-signal-review`, `recycled-hold`, `journey-stop-start`, `eliza-takeover`, `webhook-intake`, `active-member`, `route-authority`, `legacy-inbox-row`, `routing`, `open-turn-binding`, `agent-context-projection`, `participation-window`.
- **State variables.** `turn: MsgId -> {status, senderHash, leaseAt, kind, effects}`, `ledger: List[{phone, app, state, at, ref}]`, `fence: Phone -> FenceState`, `membershipState`, `memberOptedOut`, `networkOptedOut`, `pendingSignals`, `gatewayQueue`, `now`.
- **Key actions.** `sendText(kind, receivedAt)` with kinds stop, stopLikely, start, leave, help, other; `claim`, `renewLease`, `commitLedger`, `commitProjection(app)`, `collectReply`, `complete`, `throwInTurn`, `crash`, `retry`, `sweep`, `deliverResponse` (lossy, can repeat), `staffResolve`, `proposeSignal`, `tick`.
- **Invariants.** `stopNeverLost`: an accepted STOP has a ledger event, or the gateway holds a retryable answer. `stopAllApps`: a global STOP resolves `opted_out` for every app. `olderStartNoUndo`. `atMostOneProcessing` per sender. `echoMatchesLedger`: `TurnResponse.consent` equals the resolved ledger after the turn. `signalNeverActs`: a signal changes no consent. `noReplyAfterStop` except the confirmation.
- **Liveness and reachability witnesses.** Witness: a STOP is confirmed in one turn; a leave stops one app; START resumes the routed app. Liveness: `processing` always ends; projections and fence converge to the ledger; a `proposed` signal reaches a staff decision. Expected failures: SD-001, SD-002, SD-087, SD-037, SD-104, SD-024, SD-023, SD-036.
- **Tie to the code.** Each commit point of `NetworkService.inbound` and `inbox.signed` is one action, with a comment that names `service.ts` or `inbox.ts` lines. Readers are nondeterministic oracles (`stopLikely` exposes SD-031); corpus rows cover the regex. Bounds: 2 senders, 2 apps, 4 messages, 1 crash, tick 30 s, lease 4 ticks. A counterexample becomes a case in `packages/network/test/inbox-order.integration.test.ts` or `shared-agent.integration.test.ts`. Gateway behaviour is an assumption, listed as such.
- **Defects in scope (36).** SD-001, SD-002, SD-004, SD-012, SD-023, SD-024, SD-031, SD-032, SD-034, SD-036, SD-037, SD-073, SD-081, SD-087, SD-104, SD-105, SD-129, SD-130, SD-212, SD-215, SD-216, SD-217, SD-218, SD-309, SD-313, SD-314, SD-317, SD-318, SD-432, SD-433, SD-434, SD-437, SD-478, SD-490, SD-493, SD-496.

### 6.3 `quint/join_age.qnt` (P0)

- **Machines (11).** `text-join`, `web-join`, `age-floor`, `member-age-policy`, `minor-single-player`, `looking-for-enroll`, `membership`, `staff-invite`, `eliza-notice`, `member-onboarding`, `signup-channel-mode`.
- **State variables.** `pending: Phone -> Option[{app, name, age}]`, `floor: Phone -> Option[Age]`, `lowest: Person -> Option[Age]`, `everStated: Phone -> Set[Age]` (history), `statedAges` (volatile), `membership`, `memberRow`, `network: {minor, minorSignal, ageUnknown}`, `joinMode` (constant per app).
- **Key actions.** `say(age?, name?, homeOk)`, `webJoin`, `textJoin` with one step per commit, `enroll`, `noticeSend`, `unitCommit`, `statedAgeFlush`, `crash`, `ageLowered`, `staffClearMinor`, `deleteAll`, `rejoin`.
- **Invariants.** `lowestWins`: stored lowest age is at or below every stated age. `minorNeverMatched`. `under13StoresNothing` except the floor. `oneMemberPerApp`. `activeImpliesMember`. `inviteRespected`: no `active` membership without an invite on an `invite` app, except the shared ntwrk route. `everyAnswerGetsReply`. `minorNoAdultQuestion`.
- **Liveness and reachability witnesses.** Witness: adult joins by text in 3 messages; a 15-year-old joins and stays single-player; under 13 is declined. Liveness: `waitlist` reaches `active` (expected to fail, SD-295). Expected failures: SD-010, SD-011, SD-038, SD-126, SD-127, SD-128, SD-014, SD-015, SD-066.
- **Tie to the code.** Steps follow the commit list in `service.ts:1435-1463` and `accounts.ts:351-393`. Ages abstract to {none, 12, 15, 25}. Run once per `joinMode` value. Bounds: 1 phone, 2 apps, 1 crash. A counterexample becomes an integration case under `tests/e2e` or `packages/network/test`.
- **Defects in scope (24).** SD-010, SD-011, SD-014, SD-015, SD-038, SD-065, SD-066, SD-080, SD-123, SD-125, SD-126, SD-127, SD-128, SD-145, SD-179, SD-213, SD-270, SD-272, SD-276, SD-295, SD-315, SD-383, SD-436, SD-488.

### 6.4 `quint/identity_sagas.qnt` (P0)

- **Machines (6).** `person-lifecycle`, `phone-identity-hold`, `phone-change`, `ban-saga`, `erasure-saga`, `suppression`.
- **State variables.** `step: SagaId -> Int`, `residue: Person -> Set[Item]`, `people`, `phones: Phone -> {hold, lastSeen}`, `bans`, `suppression`, `memberships`, `members`, `grants`, `lineConversations`, `tombstones`, `crashAt`.
- **Key actions.** `banStep(i)`, `leaveStep(i)`, `deleteAllStep(i)`, `clearHoldStep(i, decision)`, `movePhoneTx`, `under13Decline`, `seen`, `crash`, `retry(saga)`, `concurrentPutMembership`.
- **Invariants.** `bannedNotMatchable`: a ban row implies no live opportunity and no eligibility. `residueEmptyAtDone`: at a terminal step the residue set is empty. `noPlainPhoneAfterDelete`. `retryCompletes`: every saga can finish after a crash. `noLostMembershipUpdate`. `banOnEveryPath`.
- **Liveness and reachability witnesses.** Witness: each saga reaches done with no crash; a number moves in one transaction. Liveness: every saga ends under fair retry (expected to fail for ban and clear hold). Expected failures: SD-005, SD-016, SD-029, SD-092, SD-017, SD-108, SD-109.
- **Tie to the code.** One table of paths from `implicit-sweep.md` 3.5 as a `pure def`, one step per transaction. Row locks are implicit; only gaps between transactions are explicit. Bounds: 2 persons, 2 apps, 2 phones, 1 crash. Tie to `packages/platform/src/accounts.ts`, `pg-store.ts`, `service.ts:1742-1763`; a counterexample becomes a case in `packages/platform/test/notify-retention.pg.test.ts` or a new Postgres-backed test.
- **Defects in scope (26).** SD-005, SD-016, SD-017, SD-018, SD-026, SD-029, SD-051, SD-055, SD-056, SD-092, SD-098, SD-108, SD-109, SD-124, SD-178, SD-255, SD-256, SD-274, SD-275, SD-294, SD-378, SD-379, SD-381, SD-385, SD-489, SD-492.

### 6.5 `quint/outbound_queue.qnt` (P0)

- **Machines (14).** `outbound-queue-row`, `line-drain-lease`, `cloud-deliver-handshake`, `collected-reply`, `leak-review`, `leak-gate`, `person-cap-slot`, `quiet-hours`, `live-send-gate`, `turn-receipt`, `delivery-receipts`, `line-conversation`, `line-safety`, `message-status-mirror`.
- **State variables.** `row: Id -> {status, attempts, lease, personCap, created, body, addr, kind, leakReleased}`, `conv: Addr -> {unanswered, reUsed}`, `slots: Person -> Set[Id]`, `cloudAdmitted: Set[Id]`, `cloudSent: Set[Id]`, `consent`, `erased`, `mirror: Id -> Status`, `drainer`, `liveFlags`, `now`.
- **Key actions.** `enqueue`, `gateDecide`, `admit`, `claim`, `cloudCall` (split into admit and respond), `timeout`, `acceptCommit`, `waitWrite`, `endWrite`, `recover`, `reconcileExpire`, `receiptLookup`, `collect`, `guardFail`, `receipt(outcome)`, `engaged`, `staffRelease`, `staffDrop`, `erase`, `moveNumber`, `crash`, `tick`.
- **Invariants.** `sentImpliesGates`: `sending` follows live flags, ledger, recipient, age and leak checks after the last wait. `sentAfterStop` is false. `atMostOneProviderSend` per id. `unknownNeverResends`. `noStatusRegress`. `capAtMost3`: over sent and maybe-sent rows. `slotOnlyForLiveRow`. `collectedIsNotSent`. `nonMemberBodyClearedAtEnd`. `complianceAlwaysGoes`. `mirrorMatchesQueue`.
- **Liveness and reachability witnesses.** Witness: a proactive row reaches `accepted`; a quiet-hours row is deferred then sent; a parked row is released. Liveness: every waiting row ends; `collected` leaves (expected to fail, SD-100). Expected failures: SD-090, SD-099, SD-091, SD-094, SD-101, SD-020, SD-131.
- **Tie to the code.** Gate numbers follow `outbound-queue.ts` rows 1-41 in `network-service-outbound.md` section 1. Two workers, 2 rows, 3 attempts, 3 clock buckets. The leak check is an uninterpreted oracle. Cloud is a small state machine. Counterexamples become cases in `cloud-outbound.integration.test.ts`.
- **Defects in scope (33).** SD-020, SD-057, SD-090, SD-091, SD-094, SD-099, SD-100, SD-101, SD-102, SD-103, SD-106, SD-117, SD-120, SD-131, SD-132, SD-134, SD-136, SD-147, SD-148, SD-149, SD-257, SD-277, SD-298, SD-299, SD-300, SD-304, SD-307, SD-316, SD-334, SD-335, SD-336, SD-337, SD-474.

### 6.6 `quint/opportunity.qnt` (P0)

- **Machines (12).** `send-belief`, `opportunity-lifecycle`, `opp-participant-status`, `review-item`, `awaiting-slot`, `yes-hold`, `send-pipeline-and-deferred`, `reply-reader`, `booked-attendance`, `member-request`, `outreach-attention`, `daily-gates-and-switches`.
- **State variables.** `stage: Opp -> Stage`, `status: (Opp, Member) -> PStatus`, `review: Opp -> {decision, deadline, decidedAt, invalidated}`, `awaiting: Member -> Option[{kind, opp}]`, `revealed`, `request`, `attention`, `deferred: List[Send]`, `queueTruth: Msg -> Outcome`, `belief: (Opp, Member) -> {told, toldAt, confirmed}`, `reachedByOutcome`, `now`.
- **Key actions.** `submit`, `approve`, `approveLate`, `reject`, `reroll`, `expireReview`, `probeSend`, `answerYes`, `answerNo`, `answerCounter`, `answerAmbiguous`, `answerAfterYes`, `dropMember(reason)`, `reveal`, `queueHold`, `queueExpire`, `queueDeliver`, `silenceConfirm`, `meetingPassed`, `tick`.
- **Invariants.** `reviewGate`: stage past `review` implies an approved, not invalidated decision. `decidedBeforeDeadline`. `namesAfterAllYes`: a reveal needs a real yes from every going member. `counterIsNotYes`. `slotOwnerAlive`. `noLostAnswer`. `minorNever`. `confirmedImpliesDelivered`. `withdrawnYesNotRevealed`. `terminalStable`.
- **Liveness and reachability witnesses.** Witness: approve, both yes, reveal, meeting, feedback. A reject closes with no contact. Liveness: every `review` item leaves in the SLA; a request does not stay `booked` after a closed opportunity. Expected failures: SD-041, SD-043, SD-047, SD-048, SD-009, SD-006, SD-075, SD-076, SD-077, SD-079, SD-139.
- **Tie to the code.** `dropMember` (`network.ts:2564`) is the hub, with one action per trigger. Time uses named deadlines. Reader output is an oracle with a misread. Bounds: 2 opportunities, 4 members (1 minor, 1 alternate), 1 posting slot writer. Reproduce traces with `packages/network/test/mini.ts` in integration cases, and in `packages/network/harness/scenarios.ts`.
- **Defects in scope (41).** SD-006, SD-009, SD-028, SD-030, SD-033, SD-035, SD-041, SD-043, SD-047, SD-048, SD-075, SD-076, SD-077, SD-079, SD-139, SD-140, SD-142, SD-143, SD-146, SD-219, SD-248, SD-249, SD-258, SD-259, SD-263, SD-265, SD-266, SD-267, SD-269, SD-297, SD-325, SD-326, SD-328, SD-429, SD-481, SD-482, SD-483, SD-484, SD-485, SD-486, SD-487.

### 6.18 Machines left out, with reasons

| Machines | Reason |
|---|---|
| reply readers (regex), leak guard patterns, check-in text classifier | Corpus rows and `bun run sim --only evals` test these. Quint models them as oracles only. |
| `sim-*`, `judge-*`, `oracle-decision`, `intent-liveness`, `slop-world-flow`, `peon-*`, `friends-*`, `scenario-grade`, `sim-gate-run` (sim.md, 25 machines) | The simulator is a checker, not a product. sim.md gives them no P0 or P1 rank. Its defects go to gates and judges. The proof gaps stay in the register with no module. |
| `contract-mirror` | P1. A release order for two files. `check:mirror` and pins test it. A model adds nothing. |
| `domain-enums`, `run-log`, `clock-rng-time`, `chatjson-loop` | P2. Type definitions and pure helpers, sim gates cover them. |
| `schema-catalogue` | A list of CHECK constraints. Other machines own each row. |
| `game-world`, `takeover-prompt`, `game-counters`, `shadow-run`, `lab-run`, `dev-pg`, `client-sync`, `client-decide-guard`, `delta-store`, gap-console P2 rows | P2. Simulated-world or dev-only tooling, or plain UI state. |
| `hold-queue`, `digest-slot-cursor`, `reengagement-d6`, `peon-application`, `friends-weekly-planner`, `exposure-debt` | P2. Library code with no production caller, or a value with no machine. `probe-flow` stays in `matching_engine` as P0 if wired. |
| `capital-help-confirmation`, `capital-jsonl-store`, `capital-staff-read-audit`, `capital-sim-review-loop`, `capital-launch-gates` | P2. Unused store, or simulator only. |
| `oauth-rate-window`, `oauth-audit-log`, `notify-token`, `share-grant`, `share-offer`, `rater-config`, `ledger-emitter`, `invite-growth`, `bias-group-verdict`, `image-qualification`, `plugin-snapshot` | P2. Small, append-only or derived, or no production path. `bias-group-verdict` defects need arithmetic tests, not a state model. |

## 7. Top risks

These are ordered by the rule they touch. Each one is suspected, not confirmed.

1. **STOP can be lost or delayed.** SD-001, SD-002, SD-031, SD-024. A crash after the claim gives a refused retry; a running turn refuses STOP; "STOP STOP" is not read as a stop.
2. **Consent can be assumed.** SD-041, SD-008, SD-075, SD-006. A counter reads as yes; a group falls to a named pair; a yes cannot be taken back; silence confirms a plan the member never saw.
3. **The production tick omits retention and inbox work.** SD-003. Wiring purge without care then activates SD-004.
4. **Safety does not follow the person.** SD-021, SD-022, SD-074. A leave and a rejoin clear a hold.
5. **Ban is partial.** SD-005, SD-019, SD-013, SD-073.
6. **Minor rules have gaps.** SD-010, SD-038, SD-011, SD-014, SD-065, SD-066.
7. **Number swap can go one way.** SD-007, SD-039.
8. **Staff console can act on the wrong person.** SD-068, SD-069, SD-082.

## 8. Questions for a maintainer

- Does the gateway keep its own STOP record when the backend answers `turn_unresolved`? How many retries follow `turn_busy`?
- Does an `opted_in` with scope `app` clear a fence with scope `all`? Does the fence order by `at`? Does it filter collected replies?
- Does START resume one app or all apps after a global STOP?
- May STOP always win over a newer START?
- Is one replica a firm rule, or must models use two?
- Which rule wins between the SKILL flow and the MCP contract on phone and code (SD-058)?
- Is peon and friends matching local only on the service (SD-088)?

## 9 Second research wave

Date: 2026-11-11. Branch: `claude/quint-state-machines` at 4061b99, which includes the integration branch `claude/formal-main` (LemmaScript proofs, the drift ledger `quint/drift.json`, `docs/formal-coverage.md`, `scripts/quint-drift.ts` and the Quint models). This section is research only. It changes no code and no model. Section 9.1, the rules and module scopes in 9.6, the blocker reasons and the hand-written "can be fixed now" notes in 9.4 are hand-written. The tables in 9.2 to 9.6 are generated by a script from the item list of the wave and a hand-written assignment table. The ids W3-NNN are stable from this file on. The wave is called "wave 3" in the ids because wave 1 was the first research and wave 2 was the Quint models. The plan in 9.6 is for wave 4.

**Result in numbers.** 677 items: 208 model gaps, 149 model errors, 108 open carve-outs, 38 new machines and 174 suspected defects (6 P0, 228 P1, 443 P2). Defect verdicts: 76 confirmed, 43 disputed, 55 refuted, 0 unverified. 622 items are live (all items that are not refuted defects). 37 live items are blocked (section 9.6.3). The other 585 live items are in 41 wave 4 modules: 30 extend a model in `quint/`, 11 are new.

### 9.1 Method and coverage

**Model audits (30).** One agent for each model in `quint/`. Each agent read the model, its findings file in `docs/research/quint-findings/` (the knownOpen values, the carve-outs, and the items that are skipped, not reproduced or not covered), its entry in `quint/drift.json` and in `docs/results/2026-11-09-quint-drift.md`, the LemmaScript notes in `docs/research/lemmascript/` and `docs/formal-coverage.md`, and the code that the model names. Each agent then compared the model with the code, state by state and guard by guard. The agent recorded: a model gap (the code has a state, a transition, a crash point or an actor that the model does not have), a model error (the model says something false about the code: an invariant that cannot fail, a guard that is stronger than the code, a stale line reference, a ledger row that misses a file), an open carve-out (an invariant that stays out of safety, or a defect that a model skipped), a new machine, or a suspected code defect with a trigger.

**Area sweeps (12).** `blooio-notify-core`, `concurrency`, `edge`, `engine-a`, `engine-b`, `journeys`, `mcp`, `network-core`, `network-service`, `observatory`, `platform` and `sql`. Each sweep read the code of one area across all models, to find machines that no model has and rules that fall between two models.

**Skeptics.** Two independent agents tried to refute each suspected defect against the code at this branch (348 votes). The rule is the same as in wave 1: two survivals = confirmed, one = disputed, none = refuted. When a skeptic could not show the trigger in production as deployed, the default was refuted. Some skeptics ran a throwaway script or a Postgres case in the session scratchpad; most read the code only.

**Two runs.** A usage limit stopped the first run. The first run did 17 model audits and 8 sweeps and gave W3-001 to W3-436. The second run did the other 13 audits and 4 sweeps and gave W3-437 to W3-677. The skeptic verdicts for the items of both runs were merged after the second run. The split by report:

| Run | Model audits | Sweeps | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | auth_edge, identity_sagas, join_age, matching_engine, notify_capital, oauth_mcp, opportunity, outbound_queue, photos_rating, plan_crew, plan_run, profile_learning, relay, runtime_ops, safety_trust, staff_console, turn_consent | edge, engine-a, engine-b, journeys, mcp, observatory, platform, sql | W3-001 to W3-436 |
| 2 | backup_restore, boot_config, candidate_pool, chatjson_loop, console_privacy, console_views, engine_rules, join_flow, observatory_sync, phone_identity, photo_relay_item, profile_read, review_desk | blooio-notify-core, concurrency, network-core, network-service | W3-437 to W3-677 |

**The reports.** The 42 reports are copied to [state-machines/wave3/](state-machines/wave3/) (`audit-<model>.md` and `sweep-<area>.md`). They hold the full trigger, fix and model proposal of each item. This section keeps the title, the location and a short cut.

**Limits.**

- No agent changed the repository. The scratch probes (Quint copies with a probe action, small bun scripts) stay in the session scratchpad. They are not in the repository, so a probe result is evidence for research only, not PR evidence.
- Most audits read the branch before the merge of `claude/formal-main`. Items that say "fixed only on claude/formal-main" or "the HEAD model is stale" can be closed by the merge. A wave 4 author must check each such item against the merged model first (for example W3-131, W3-100, W3-037, W3-326, W3-348).
- A file:line is from the report. Lines can move. Read the code before acting.
- The verdict reasons in 9.5 are extracted by a script from the first 500 characters of each vote. A reason can stop mid-sentence. The full votes are in the session scratchpad (`items.json`), not in the repository.
- Some items are duplicates found by two agents (for example W3-021 and W3-645, W3-141 and W3-650, W3-303 and W3-323, W3-429 and W3-666, W3-646, W3-662 and W3-665, W3-357 and W3-672). The plan puts each pair in the same module.
- The gateway and Eliza Cloud are upstream. Statements about them are assumptions.

### 9.3 Model gaps and model errors per model

Gap = the code has it, the model does not. Error = the model says something false about the code (a vacuous or tautological invariant, a guard stronger than the code, a stale fact, a ledger miss). Items from a sweep are listed under the model of their wave 4 module. "What to add" is a cut of the proposal of the report.

### 9.6 Wave 4 module plan

#### 9.6.1 Rules for wave 4

- The rules of section 6.1 and `quint/README.md` stay. One file per module. No module imports another model.
- Each live item (gap, error, carve-out, new machine, confirmed or disputed defect) is in exactly one module or in the blocked list (9.6.3). Refuted defects are in no module.
- A module extends the existing `quint/<k>.qnt` when the machine belongs there. A new module is a new file only when it is a separate machine. When a big model had more than about 25 items, its items were split only where they touch a different machine: `peon_postings` from `matching_engine`, `cloud_auth` from `auth_edge`, `mcp_authorize` from `oauth_mcp`, `migrations` from `runtime_ops`, `shared_actions` from `relay` and `turn_consent`.
- For a disputed defect, reproduce it first (a run test in the model, or an integration or e2e case). If the trigger cannot be shown, record the result in the findings file and do not fix the code.
- A confirmed defect that the model reproduces needs an integration, e2e, sim gate or corpus case that fails before the fix (docs/tests-policy.md). Both levels change in the same PR (AGENTS.md formal modeling requirement): the Quint model and the LemmaScript core, with the same invariant name.
- After each module: compare the model with the code, then `bun run quint:check quint/<k>.qnt` and `bun run quint:drift --stamp quint/<k>.qnt` (with `--drift` for a known drift that the PR does not fix).
- Items that a merge of `claude/formal-main` can have closed (9.1, limits) are checked first and closed with a note in the findings file.
- A fix in a console or site client is a broken-flow fix and needs before and after video (AGENTS.md UI rule).
